Psychomotor Retardation (Motor): Visible slowing of general body movements, such as walking speed or picking up objects.\n  - Psychomotor Retardation (Speech): Long response latency or slowed speech when asked a question.\n  - Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  Duration: 1 year and 5 months\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Chronic Fatigue: Fatigue that persists for a long period even after adequate rest.\n  - Physical Fatigue: A state of physical exhaustion where the body feels heavy and easily tires even from minor activities.\n  - Mental Fatigue: A state where fatigue accumulates rapidly during the process of thinking or concentrating.\n  - Loss of Energy: Difficulty performing daily activities due to a decline in energy and vitality.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  Duration: 1 year and 5 months\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Candidate Diseases: \n[\"Major Depressive Disorder with Psychotic Features\"]\n\nInterview Transcript: \nDoctor: Hello. I'm glad you're here today. What brings you in to see me?\n\nPatient: I've just been feeling incredibly down and sad for about a year and a half now, and it never seems to go away. It's gotten to the point where I just can't shake this heavy, depressed mood anymore. That's really why I finally decided to seek help.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods lasting at least a few days where you felt the opposite of depressed—like your mood was unusually high, you had racing thoughts, or you felt like you didn't need to sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. I have really only felt depressed and low, and I've never had those kinds of high-energy periods. It's just been a constant heavy feeling.\n\nDoctor: Along with feeling down, have you also been struggling with excessive, uncontrollable worry or anxiety about everyday things?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I struggle with that kind of constant worry. It's not really anxiety that's the problem, but more just a deep, heavy sadness that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: During this past year and a half, have you had any periods lasting two months or longer where you felt completely back to your normal, non-depressed self?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any breaks like that at all. The depression and low energy have been completely continuous and constant for the entire year and five months. I haven't felt like my normal self since this all started.\n\nDoctor: Have you also lost interest or pleasure in doing things you used to enjoy, or is it mostly just the low mood you're experiencing?\n\nPatient: It's definitely more of the persistent low mood and a deep, constant sadness. I feel this heavy emptiness and emotional numbness inside, rather than just a loss of interest in things. It just feels like my emotions are completely flat and hollow all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period earlier in your life where you felt this same kind of low mood continuously for two full years or longer?\n\nPatient: No, I don't recall ever experiencing a low mood that lasted that long in the past. This current struggle has been going on for about a year and a half, but before this, I've never had anything last for two years or more.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been during this time—are you having trouble sleeping, or perhaps sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I haven't noticed any major changes in my sleep patterns. I seem to sleep about the same as I always have, so I don't think that has been a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently heard or seen things that others couldn't, or held strong beliefs that other people found unusual or unrealistic?\n\nPatient: No, I don't hear or see things that aren't there, but I do hold a strong belief that others tell me makes no sense. I feel deeply responsible for all the terrible misfortunes and disasters happening in the world, and I truly believe I deserve to be punished for them.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight since this low mood started, or has that remained mostly the same?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed any significant changes in my appetite or my weight. They have both remained pretty much the same since this all started.\n\nDoctor: Did you start believing you were responsible for these world disasters only after you began feeling this deep sadness, or did you hold this belief even during times when you weren't feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes, I only started believing that after this deep sadness and depression began about a year and five months ago. I never held those beliefs or felt that way before this heavy mood took over. It definitely started once the depression set in."
  }

Concurrently, they experience mood-congruent delusions of guilt and deserved punishment (believing they are responsible for world disasters), which began only after the onset of the depressive symptoms, supporting a diagnosis of Major Depressive Disorder with Psychotic Features."}
